And today I want to simplify things even further. Despite the thousands of diet books and programs out there, every diet really falls into just three basic types. CR, DR, and TR. Now, CR is calorie restriction. This is where you weigh and measure your foods, read labels, and possibly log everything you eat into a meal tracker to make sure you're in a calorie deficit. Now, DR is dietary restriction. These diets restrict certain food types, such as how keto eliminates carbs, paleo cuts out everything a caveman didn't eat, and the vegan avoids animal products. Now, TR is time restriction. This is when you eat only during specific windows of time, such as intermittent fasting, like a 16-8 window, or alternate day fasting, or extended fasts. Now, as I often say, there's many paths to the same destination, and any of these approaches can work, and many people combine them. For example, some might track their calories, calorie restriction, and also reduce carbs, dietary restriction, and only eat during an eight-hour feeding window. Time restriction. But again, success depends on finding the diet that works best for you, the one that fits your lifestyle, the diet that feels the least restrictive to you, and sticking with it for a long time. So once you actually do calculate that number, understand that it is an estimate, you do need to create a deficit by reducing calories in order to lose weight or create a surplus by adding calories in order to gain weight.
